]{Listing\lx@nobreakspace 32}{The object-oriented fluent API in \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{listing:fluent:dyck} and \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:functional:dyck} converted to SML}}}}}}\par ThegeneralDCFLencodingalgorithmispresentedin\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{algorithm:tabulation:dcfl}.ThisalgorithmstartsbyapplyingGilandRoth^{\prime}sconstructiontoproduceanobject-orientedfluentAPI,andthenappliesthe(modified)tabulationencodingtoremoveoverloading.\par\begin{algorithm}\@@toccaption{{\lx@tag[ ]{{4}}{The tabulation algorithm for encoding a DPDA as an SML fluent API}}}\@@caption{{\lx@tag[: ]{{Algorithm 4}} {The tabulation algorithm for encoding a DPDA as an SML fluent API}}}
\centering\fcolorbox{black!25}{white}{
\begin{minipage}{.91\textwidth}
\begin{algorithmic}[1]
\INPUT DPDA $A$
\STATE Apply the tree encoding algorithm of \citet{Gil:2019} to convert $A$ into an object-oriented fluent API $P$ as specified in \cref{eq:function,eq:initial,eq:termination}.
\PRINT{\inline[style=sml,literate=*{D}{\textdollar}{1}]{datatype DD = DD}}
\STATE{Enumerate $P$'s API functions \cref{eq:function} $R=\{\rho_1, \rho_2, \ldots, \rho_n\}$}
\FORALL{$j=1, 2, \ldots, n$}
\PRINT{\inline[style=sml,literate=*{E}{\hbox to 1em{.\hss.\hss.}}{1}]{fun I$j$ (x1, x2, E, x$n$) = x$j$}}
\ENDFOR\STATE{Enumerate $P's$ API methods $\Sigma=\{\sigma_1, \sigma_2, \ldots, \sigma_m\}$}
\STATE{Enumerate $P$'s API types $\Gamma=\{\gamma_1, \gamma_2, \ldots, \gamma_l\}$}
\FORALL{$i=1, 2, \ldots, l$}
\LET{$a$}{\inline[literate=*{D}{\textdollar}{1}]{DD} if $P$ includes the method $\text{\texttt{\textdollar}}:\gamma_i(\boldsymbol{x})\rightarrow\text{\texttt{()}}$ or else \inline{()}}
\FORALL{$j=1, 2, \ldots, m$}
\LET{$I^{(j)}$}{$\text{\inline{I$k$}}$ where $\rho_k=(\sigma_j:\gamma_i(\boldsymbol{x})\rightarrow\tau)\in P$}
\LET{$I^{(j)}$}{$\text{\inline{()}}$ where $(\sigma_j:\gamma_i(\boldsymbol{x})\rightarrow\tau)$ is not defined in $P$}
\ENDFOR\IF{$\gamma_i$ is not generic}
\PRINT{\inline[style=sml,literate=*{E}{\hbox to 1em{.\hss.\hss.}}{1}]{val\ $\gamma_i$ = ($a$,\ ($I^{(1)}$,\ $I^{(2)}$, E,\ $I^{(m)}$), ())}}
\ELSE\PRINT{\inline[style=sml,literate=*{E}{\hbox to 1em{.\hss.\hss.}}{1}]{fun\ $\gamma_i$ ($\boldsymbol{x}$) = ($a$,\ ($I^{(1)}$,\ $I^{(2)}$, E,\ $I^{(m)}$), ($\boldsymbol{x}$))}}
\ENDIF\ENDFOR\FORALL{$i=1, 2, \ldots, n$}
\LET{$(\sigma:\gamma(\boldsymbol{x})\rightarrow\tau)$}{$\rho_i$}
\PRINT{\inline{r$i$ ($\boldsymbol{x}$) =\ $\tau$}}
\ENDFOR\PRINT{\inline[style=sml,literate=*{E}{\hbox to 1em{.\hss.\hss.}}{1}]{val r = (r1, r2, E, r$n$)}}
\PRINT{\inline[style=sml,literate=*{C}{\textasciicircum}{1}]{fun CC f' = f'\ $t_0$} where $\text{\lstinline/\^\^/}:t_0$ is $P$'s initial variable}
\FORALL{$j=1, 2, \ldots, m$}
\PRINT{\inline[style=sml,literate=*{E}{\hbox to 1em{.\hss.\hss.}}{1}]{val\ $\sigma_j$ (_, (_, E, I, E, _), X) f' = f' (I r X)} where the inner tuple has $m$ entries and argument \inline{I} appears in the $j$\hh entry}
\ENDFOR\PRINT{\inline[style=sml,literate=*{D}{\textdollar}{1}{E}{\hbox to 1em{.\hss.\hss.}}{1}]{fun D (DD, _, _) = ()}}
\end{algorithmic}
\end{minipage}}\@add@centering\end{algorithm}\par\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{algorithm:tabulation:dcfl}acceptsasinputaDPDAacceptingthetargetDCFL.Inline1,itconvertstheinputintoanobject-orientedfluentAPIusingtreeencoding.Inlines8through16thealgorithmencodestheAPItypesasfunctionsandvariables.Thetreerewriterulesareencodedinlines17through20.Thetreepattern\tau isprintedas-is.Finally,thealgorithmprintstheAPIfunctionsinlines21through24.\par\par\@@numbered@section{section}{toc}{Discussion}
\par\@@numbered@section{subsection}{toc}{Functional vs.~Object-Oriented Fluent APIs}
\par Functional fluent APIs share many qualities with their object-oriented counterparts.
They provide a fluent DSL embedding method that employs a simple and uniform syntax devoid of host language control structures.
The simplicity of fluent API designs makes them compatible with a wide range of functional host languages.
Our encoding algorithms in \lx@cref{creftypeplural\lx@tilde refnum}{section:shuffling}, \lx@cref{refnum}{section:church}, \lx@cref{refnum}{section:tabulation} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{section:beyond} show that functional fluent API constructions can be generalized to entire classes of DSLs, namely all regular languages and DCFLs.
These results are in line with recent advances in the study of object-oriented fluent APIs, focusing on fluent APIs for DCFLs \cite[citep]{(\@@bibref{AuthorsPhrase1Year}{Gil:2016,Gil:2019,Yamazaki:2019}{\@@citephrase{, }}{})}.
\par Nevertheless, there are two notable differences between object-oriented and functional fluent APIs.
First, the functional fluent style is cleaner because functions are called without the dot and parenthesis (\hbox{\pagecolor{lighter-grey}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor{\@listingGroup{}{$\mathstrut$}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{x}}}.{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{f}}}()}}}}) common to object-oriented languages.
This removes the last bit of host language syntax from the EDSL.
In combination with operator overloading, found in many popular functional languages, functional fluent APIs can produce extremely accurate DSL embeddings, as demonstrated by our HTML example in \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{section:aa}.
\par The second difference relates to embedding of DSL literals.
DSL literals are embedded as function arguments in the fluent API.
In our embedded HTML document in \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{listing:html}, for example, attribute \hbox{\pagecolor{lighter-grey}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor{\@listingGroup{}{$\mathstrut$}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{src}}}}}}} in the \hbox{\pagecolor{lighter-grey}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor{\@listingGroup{}{$\mathstrut$}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{img}}}}}}} tag receives a string argument specifying the image source:
\par{\@listings{\@@listings@block{33}{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@numbers\footnotesize\@lst@startline{}\textless{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{img}}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{src}}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_string}{\color[rgb]{0.16,0,1}{"https://tinyurl.com/yosemite5"}}}/\textgreater\@lst@endline}}{}\@@toccaption{{\lx@tag[ ]{{33}}{The object-oriented fluent API in \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{listing:fluent:dyck} and \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:functional:dyck} converted to SML}}}\@@caption{{\lx@tag[: ]{Listing\lx@nobreakspace 33}{The object-oriented fluent API in \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{listing:fluent:dyck} and \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:functional:dyck} converted to SML}}}}}}Although adding parameters to our fluent API designs is a technical matter, it has an inherent limitation.
The lack of overloading in functional settings means that the fluent APIs cannot overload argument types.
For instance, \hbox{\pagecolor{lighter-grey}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor{\@listingGroup{}{$\mathstrut$}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{img}}}}}}} can receive a string \emph{or} a URL object, but not both.
This detail could possibly hinder certain EDSL designs and should be kept in mind when making functional fluent APIs.
\par\par\@@numbered@section{subsection}{toc}{A Plethora of Functional Fluent API Encoding Methods}
\par\lx@cref{creftypepluralcap\lx@tilde refnum}{section:shuffling}, \lx@cref{refnum}{section:church} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{section:tabulation} present no less than three different algorithms for encoding regular languages as functional fluent APIs.
The most promising method is probably bit shuffling, described in \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{section:shuffling}.
Bit shuffling fluent APIs do not employ unification, so they are expected to induce reasonable compilation times across various languages and compilers.
The bit shuffling improvements described in \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{section:shuffling:improvements} greatly enhance utility by reducing code size and improving error reporting.
\par The crux of the bit shuffling construction is to describe the FSM as a collection of circuits or logic gates.
In \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{section:church} we showed how to implement these circuits at the type level using Church Booleans and \cite[citeauthor]{\@@bibref{Authors}{Mairson:2004}{}{}}'s~[\cite[citeyearpar]{(\@@bibref{Year}{Mairson:2004}{}{})}] fanout gate.
Compile-time circuits may have some additional implications for API design beyond fluent API.
For instance, they can be used to conduct numeric calculations in a type-safe physical dimensions library \cite[citep]{(\@@bibref{AuthorsPhrase1Year}{Kennedy:1994}{\@@citephrase{, }}{})}.
\par Tabulation encoding (\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{section:tabulation}) is a flexible embedding method that can be generalized for non-regular DSLs (\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{section:beyond}).
It provides the theoretical basis for a functional fluent API generator in the style of Fling \cite[citep]{(\@@bibref{AuthorsPhrase1Year}{Gil:2019,Roth:2019}{\@@citephrase{, }}{})} and TypeLevelLR \cite[citep]{(\@@bibref{AuthorsPhrase1Year}{Yamazaki:2019}{\@@citephrase{, }}{})}.
Such tools compile LL, LALR, and LR grammars into fluent APIs that validate the DSL syntax at compile time and produce abstract syntax trees (ASTs) at run time.
\par\par\@@numbered@section{subsection}{toc}{Functional Subchaining}
\par A fluent API embeds a DSL program as a single chain of method calls.
This design can be limiting when we want to create \emph{excerpts} of the DSL program.
In the HTML document of \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{listing:html}, we might decide, for example, to place the HTML table in a variable and then reference it in the complete webpage.
Recently, \cite[citet]{\@@bibref{Authors Phrase1YearPhrase2}{Yamazaki:2022}{\@@citephrase{(}}{\@@citephrase{)}}} presented a new generation method for object-oriented fluent APIs that supports \emph{subchains}---fluent chains that embed DSL fragments.
Since our functional fluent API designs use curried functions, they come with subchaining (almost) out of the box.
\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{listing:subchaining} demonstrates functional subchaining using our HTML fluent API.
\par\par{\@listings{\@@listings@block{34}{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\footnotesize\@lst@startline{\lx@tags{\lx@tag@intags[]{{1}}\lx@tag@intags[autoref]{1\hbox{}}\lx@tag@intags[creftype]{}\lx@tag@intags[creftypecap]{}\lx@tag@intags[creftypeplural]{}\lx@tag@intags[creftypepluralcap]{}\lx@tag@intags[refnum]{{1}}\lx@tag@intags[typerefnum]{{line }}\textless{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{html}}}\textgreater\@lst@endline\@lst@startline{\lx@tags{\lx@tag@intags[]{{9}}\lx@tag@intags[autoref]{9\hbox{}}\lx@tag@intags[creftype]{}\lx@tag@intags[creftypecap]{}\lx@tag@intags[creftypeplural]{}\lx@tag@intags[creftypepluralcap]{}\lx@tag@intags[refnum]{{9}}\lx@tag@intags[typerefnum]{{line usage*)}}}\@lst@endline\@lst@startline{\lx@tags{\lx@tag@intags[]{{11}}\lx@tag@intags[autoref]{11\hbox{}}\lx@tag@intags[creftype]{}\lx@tag@intags[creftypecap]{}\lx@tag@intags[creftypeplural]{}\lx@tag@intags[creftypepluralcap]{}\lx@tag@intags[refnum]{{11}}\lx@tag@intags[typerefnum]{{line }}\textless/{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{body}}}\textgreater\@lst@endline\@lst@startline{\lx@tags{\lx@tag@intags[]{{12}}\lx@tag@intags[autoref]{12\hbox{}}\lx@tag@intags[creftype]{}\lx@tag@intags[creftypecap]{}\lx@tag@intags[creftypeplural]{}\lx@tag@intags[creftypepluralcap]{}\lx@tag@intags[refnum]{{12}}\lx@tag@intags[typerefnum]{{line 13}}}}\textbackslash{\@listingGroup{}{$\ $}}\@lst@endline}}{}\@@toccaption{{\lx@tag[ ]{{34}}{Subchaining with a functional fluent API}}}\@@caption{{\lx@tag[: ]{Listing\lx@nobreakspace 34}{Subchaining with a functional fluent API}}}}}}\par Function \hbox{\pagecolor{lighter-grey}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor{\@listingGroup{}{$\mathstrut$}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{sc}}}}}}}, defined in line 1 of \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{listing:subchaining}, is an auxiliary function for creating subchains.
Our fluent API designs require no additions or modifications to support subchaining besides \hbox{\pagecolor{lighter-grey}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor{\@listingGroup{}{$\mathstrut$}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{sc}}}}}}}.
In lines 2 through 6, we define an HTML excerpt containing an HTML table.
The boilerplate code
\begin{quote}\centering\hbox{\pagecolor{lighter-grey}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor{\@listingGroup{}{$\mathstrut$}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_keyword}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.33}{fn}}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{f}}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_string}{\color[rgb]{0.16,0,1}{\textquoteright{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}=\textgreater{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}sc{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}f\textquoteright}}}}}}}\@add@centering\end{quote}in line 2 must precede every subchain.
Variable \hbox{\pagecolor{lighter-grey}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor{\@listingGroup{}{$\mathstrut$}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{table}}}}}}} containing the HTML table is used as part of a complete HTML embedding in lines 7 through 13.
\par At the type level, using a subchain is equivalent to inlining its code, so the fluent API correctly reports API misuses in subchains.
However, the error messages are displayed where the subchain is referenced and not at the error's actual location.
We speculate that more sophisticated fluent API designs could force the compiler to check the correctness of subchains at the place of definition.
\par\par\@@numbered@section{subsection}{toc}{Implementation and Experiments}
\par This work is accompanied by \emph{Flunct} \cite[citep]{(\@@bibref{AuthorsPhrase1Year}{Roth:2023:artifact}{\@@citephrase{, }}{})}, an SML implementation of the bit shuffling, Church, and tabulation encoding techniques.
Flunct compiles a given FSM $M$ into a functional fluent API that validates $M$ at compile time.
In \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{section:flunct} we demonstrate how Flunct can be used to embed a DSL in SML.
We used Flunct to conduct a series of experiments to measure the code size and compilation time of the various fluent API constructions described in the paper.
The results of these experiments are described in \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{section:experiments}.

\par\par\endthebibliography\par\par\par\lx@newpage\par\par\par\@@numbered@section{appendix}{toc}{Flunct---a Functional Fluent API Generator}
Flunct embeds a DSL specified by an FSM as a functional fluent API using the bit shuffling, Church Boolean, and tabulation techniques described in \lx@cref{creftypeplural\lx@tilde refnum}{section:shuffling}, \lx@cref{refnum}{section:church} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{section:tabulation}.
In this appendix, we show how to use Flunct to embed a DSL in SML.
The running example DSL is the \emph{Command-Line Interface (CLI) Builder}, a DSL for creating command-line applications.
For instance, we can use the CLI Builder DSL to create an ``ice cream machine'' command-line application:

flavors}}\@lst@endline\@lst@startline{\lx@tags{\lx@tag@intags[]{{9}}\lx@tag@intags[autoref]{9\hbox{}}\lx@tag@intags[creftype]{}\lx@tag@intags[creftypecap]{}\lx@tag@intags[creftypeplural]{}\lx@tag@intags[creftypepluralcap]{}\lx@tag@intags[refnum]{{9}}\lx@tag@intags[typerefnum]{{line 9}}}}{\@listingGroup{}{$\color[rgb]{0.4,0.4,0.4}\ldots$}}\@lst@endline}}{}\@@toccaption{{\lx@tag[ ]{{35}}{The ice cream machine CLI}}}\@@caption{{\lx@tag[: ]{Listing\lx@nobreakspace 35}{The ice cream machine CLI}}}}}}\par The CLI Builder supports the following properties:
\begin{enumerate}\enumerate@item@{name}: Application name.
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@{description}: Application description (optional).
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@A list of zero or more arguments comprising:
\begin{enumerate}\enumerate@item@{argument}: Argument name.
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@{optional}: Makes the argument optional (optional).
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@{alias}: A list of zero or more argument aliases.
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@{argtype}: The type of values accepted by the argument (optional); has a list of zero or more type aliases:
\begin{enumerate}\enumerate@item@{alias}: If specified, the argument type is an enumeration of all type aliases.
\end{enumerate}\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@{description}: Argument description (optional).
\end{enumerate}\end{enumerate}We can formally define the CLI Builder DSL using a regular expression:
\begin{equation*}\texttt{name description? (optional? argument alias${}^{*}$ (argtype alias${}^{*}$)? description)${}^{*}$}\end{equation*}Flunct does not support regular expressions at present.
Hence, the first step in embedding the CLI Builder DSL is to encode it as an FSM, as shown in \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{figure:cli:fsm}.

\@add@centering\end{figure}\par We can use Flunct's interface to describe the FSM of \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{figure:cli:fsm} in SML.
First, we write the FSM alphabet:

\hbox{\pagecolor{lighter-grey}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor{\@listingGroup{}{$\mathstrut$}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{Letter}}}}}}} constructor accepts the API function name and the list of its argument types.
Most functions in our CLI Builder example accept a single \hbox{\pagecolor{lighter-grey}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor{\@listingGroup{}{$\mathstrut$}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{string}}}}}}} argument.
Notice that we chose to encode the {alias} function as an operator, \hbox{\pagecolor{lighter-grey}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor{\@listingGroup{}{$\mathstrut$}}\textbar\textbar}}}}.

Each transition comprises a source state, input letter, and a target state.
For example, line 7 defines that the FSM moves from state $q_{1}$ to state $q_{3}$ when reading {optional}.
\par To convert the FSM into an SML fluent API we simply call
\par{\@listings{\@@listings@block{38}{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@numbers\footnotesize\@lst@startline{}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_keyword}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.33}{print}}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}({\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{flunct\textunderscore api}}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_string}{\color[rgb]{0.16,0,1}{"CLI"}}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{fsm}}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{Sparse}}})\@lst@endline}}{}\@@toccaption{{\lx@tag[ ]{{38}}{The object-oriented fluent API in \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{listing:fluent:dyck} and \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:functional:dyck} converted to SML}}}\@@caption{{\lx@tag[: ]{Listing\lx@nobreakspace 38}{The object-oriented fluent API in \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{listing:fluent:dyck} and \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:functional:dyck} converted to SML}}}}}}The last argument specifies the encoding method.
Flunct supports four methods: \hbox{\pagecolor{lighter-grey}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor{\@listingGroup{}{$\mathstrut$}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{Shuffle}}}}}}} (bit shuffling), \hbox{\pagecolor{lighter-grey}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor{\@listingGroup{}{$\mathstrut$}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{Church}}}}}}} (Church Booleans), \hbox{\pagecolor{lighter-grey}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor{\@listingGroup{}{$\mathstrut$}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{Tabulation}}}}}}}, and \hbox{\pagecolor{lighter-grey}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor{\@listingGroup{}{$\mathstrut$}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{Sparse}}}}}}} (improved bit shuffling as per \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{section:shuffling:improvements}).
Running this line produces a complete fluent API for the CLI builder in \hbox{\pagecolor{lighter-grey}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor{\@listingGroup{}{$\mathstrut$}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_keyword}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.33}{struct}}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{CLI}}}}}}}.

If, for instance, we write \hbox{\pagecolor{lighter-grey}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor{\@listingGroup{}{$\mathstrut$}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{optional}}}}}}} twice in line 10, the code does not compile:
\par{\@listings{\@@listings@block{40}{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\footnotesize\@lst@startline{\lx@tags{\lx@tag@intags[]{{1}}\lx@tag@intags[autoref]{1\hbox{}}\lx@tag@intags[creftype]{}\lx@tag@intags[creftypecap]{}\lx@tag@intags[creftypeplural]{}\lx@tag@intags[creftypepluralcap]{}\lx@tag@intags[refnum]{{1}}\lx@tag@intags[typerefnum]{{line }}icecream{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}flavors"){\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}optional){\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}optional\textquoteright}}}\@lst@endline}}{}\@@toccaption{{\lx@tag[ ]{{40}}{The object-oriented fluent API in \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{listing:fluent:dyck} and \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:functional:dyck} converted to SML}}}\@@caption{{\lx@tag[: SML}}}}}}\par The embedded program produces a list of tokens:

\par\par\@@numbered@section{appendix}{toc}{Code Size and Compilation Time Experiments}
This appendix describes a series of experiments measuring the code size and compilation time of the different fluent API constructions described throughout the paper.
Most of the experiments were conducted using Flunct, our functional fluent API generator introduced in \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{section:flunct}.
The purpose of these experiments is to give us a general idea about the feasibility of fluent APIs in different situations.
In particular, the results may vary when switching the underlying DSLs.
\par\emph{A logarithmic scale is applied to all graph axes in this appendix.}
All compilation times are averaged over \emph{five} runs.
\par\par\@@numbered@section{subsection}{toc}{The Ring Experiment}
\par A \emph{ring} is an FSM with $n$ states that recognizes the language $(a^{n})^{*}$, as depicted in \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{figure:ring}.
The ring's states are organized in a circle with each state having a single edge to the state on its right, and the initial state being the only accepting state.
For example, the article's running example FSM from \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{figure:fsm:a4} is a 4-state ring.
We use rings as a simple and scalable FSM design.

\@@toccaption{{\lx@tag[ ]{{B.1}}{A ring FSM with $n$ states}}}\@@caption{{\lx@tag[. ]{{\small Figure B.1}}{\small A ring FSM with $n$ states}}}
\@add@centering\end{figure}\par In the ring experiment, we compiled rings of different sizes into fluent APIs using different encoding methods.
Recall that the \emph{Sparse} encoding method is bit shuffling augmented by the improvements described in \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{section:shuffling:improvements}.
We then measured the compilation times of these fluent APIs on chains of various length with different compilers and languages.
A \emph{chain} of length $k$ is a fluent expression with $k$ calls to \hbox{\pagecolor{lighter-grey}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor{\@listingGroup{}{$\mathstrut$}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{a}}}}}}}:
\begin{equation*}\text{\hbox{\pagecolor{lighter-grey}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@rulecolor{\@listingGroup{}{$\mathstrut$}}\@listingLiterate{\textasciicircum}\@listingLiterate{\textasciicircum}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ \@@toccaption{{\lx@tag[ ]{{B.5}}{Chain length vs.~compilation time with 4-state Ring FSM and SML/NJ}}}\@@caption{{\lx@tag[. ]{{\small Figure B.5}}{\small Chain length vs.~compilation time with 4-state Ring FSM and SML/NJ}}}
\@add@centering\end{figure}\par\par\lx@newpage\par\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{figure:ring:size} depicts the fluent APIs' code size.
As expected, the size of bit shuffling fluent APIs is exponential in the ring size.
There are two variables to be considered when measuring compilation time: the FSM size and chain length.
\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{figure:ring:time:1} shows the compilation times of chains of various lengths using the MLton SML compiler and a constant 16-state ring.
\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{figure:ring:time:2} depicts the compilation times of the empty chain (zero length) on rings of various sizes using MLton.
The SML/NJ interpreter is not quite suitable for these kind of experiment since it cannot function as a compiler.
Nevertheless, it is possible to approximate the compilation time of SML/NJ on \emph{individual expressions}.
\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{figure:ring:time:1:smlnj} shows the compilation times of chains of various lengths using the SML/NJ interpreter and a constant 4-state ring.
These compilation times are calculated on the chains alone, without the fluent APIs.
Note that SML/NJ gets stuck for quite some time when compiling chains longer than the ones used in \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{figure:ring:time:1:smlnj}.
\par\par\@@numbered@section{subsection}{toc}{The HTML Experiment}
\par In this experiment we measured the compilation time of our HTML API, introduced in \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{section:aa}.
As explained in \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{section:beyond}, the HTML fluent API is the product of a bit shuffling API with a 61-state FSM and several stack machines.
We used this API to embed the HTML webpage in \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{listing:html} and added the following table after line 15:

\par\par\@@numbered@section{subsection}{toc}{The DCFL Experiment}
\par In this experiment we measured the compilation time of the fluent API in \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{listing:functional:dyck:2} encoding a DCFL.
Recall that this fluent API was created using (extended) tabulation encoding as described in \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{section:beyond}.
We used chains of variable lengths and repeated the experiment in two languages:
Once in SML with MLton and once in the ELM programming language \cite[citep]{(\@@bibref{AuthorsPhrase1Year}{Czaplicki:2013}{\@@citephrase{, }}{})} using the ELM compiler.
The results are shown in \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{figure:dcfl:time}.
\par\par\@@numbered@section{subsection}{toc}{Technical Details}
\par The experiments described in this appendix were conducted on a (fairly old) laptop with the following properties:
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@Operating system: Ubuntu 20.04.
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@Processor: Intel(R) Core(TM) i7-5600U CPU @ 2.60GHz
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@MLton version: 20130715
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@SML/NJ version: v110.79
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@ELM version: 0.19.1
